The pipeline now defines a security-report-summary job to download the Gitleaks, Semgrep, and Trivy artifacts and emit only rule IDs, severities, and file basenames, never finding values or full paths; this job has not yet run. The GitGuardian job remains conditional on GITGUARDIAN_API_KEY, which is not configured; the available Gmail searches found no API-key message. No repository data was sent to GitGuardian.

No GPU, VM, driver, firmware, module, or boot state changed in this increment. The experimental NVIDIA bundle remains uninstalled and unloaded. No same-process V100 + RTX 5080 CUDA execution is validated.

Two isolated Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` builds with the same GCC 15.2 configuration provide a discriminating A/B: without the patch, objtool reported **2,685** RETHUNK warnings (2,936 objtool warnings total); with the return-thunk patch, it reported **zero** RETHUNK warnings but **1,951 other objtool warnings** (1,715 frame-pointer call warnings and 236 BP-scratch warnings). A third probe adding `-fno-omit-frame-pointer` emitted zero objtool warnings, but its runtime/performance cost is unmeasured and the extra flag is not part of the retained patch. The patch's unified-diff context was shortened without changing the source postimage; the final exact patch replay is tested. [Sanitized comparison](../evidence/r595112-modeset-rethunk-ab-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 `6af94199de158c343557de3aef6dc5a5e799ce4039d409ba0b0e4e44903a793e`.

This modeset experiment is adjacent to, not part of, the selected four-module compute bundle: its static manifest has `nvidia`, `hybrid580_legacy`, `hybrid580_open`, and `nvidia_uvm`, with no `nvidia-modeset`. In the reduced build, the renamed `hybrid580_open` provider does not export the stock `nvidia_get_rm_ops` symbol expected by the R595 modeset module; `KBUILD_MODPOST_WARN=1` allowed objtool comparison to complete. Do not treat this result as V100 hybrid evidence. No module was installed or loaded and no GPU/runtime/performance test ran. Raw logs and `.ko` artifacts stay under ignored `build/experiments/`.

## Current increment — R595 P2P producer/consumer direction audit — 2026-11-02T21:58Z

The pinned open R595.99.02 and R595.112.07 RM source maps `bar1PcieAtomics[0]` to device1 accessing device2 BAR1, and index 1 to the reverse direction. RM gates the query on read+write access and BAR1 support. Its atomic helper checks only the BAR1-owning GPU architecture (Blackwell GB1xx or newer), ignores the mapping-GPU argument, and retains a TODO for PCIe topology/routing. UVM orders device handles by parent ID before the RM call and uses index 0 lower-ID→higher-ID, index 1 higher-ID→lower-ID. The nearby `gpu[i] -> gpu[i-1]` comment remains ambiguous and is not used as proof.

The sanitized [source receipt](../evidence/r595-p2p-producer-v2-20261102.json), SHA-256 `342152cf82b334c141efcdd97d64d87abc3629e8906e65b75ba50dce34f2f19b`, pins both upstream commits and exact source hashes. ## Current increment — R580/R595 P2P source ABI differential — 2026-11-02T17:05Z

The exact NVIDIA R580.196.07 and R595.112.07 MIT source archives expose a concrete UVM payload delta. R580's `UvmGpuP2PCapsParams` contains the common fields through two BAR1 DMA addresses and sizes. R595 appends `NvBool bar1PcieAtomics[2]`; both releases define `NvBool` as `NvU8`. The reproducible C layout probe reports 64-byte / 8-byte-aligned R580 and 72-byte / 8-byte-aligned R595 structs, with the R595 atomic flags at offset 64. R580 labels its PCIe result `UVM_LINK_TYPE_PCIE` even when BAR1 DMA mappings are returned; R595 distinguishes `UVM_LINK_TYPE_PCIE_BAR1` from ordinary PCIe.

Added [`audit_p2p_branch_delta.py`](../tools/audit_p2p_branch_delta.py), source excerpts retaining NVIDIA's MIT notices, and [receipt](../evidence/r580-r595-p2p-abi-delta-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `dfd5c33957f54fbe300ae4d9d8d0c41568a71233acb553daa5ce623d8d8a57c8`. Eight source contracts and the C ABI probe pass. The existing R580→R595 UVM converter already reads the 64-byte legacy shape, translates the link type from the BAR1 ranges, copies the common fields, and clears both newer atomic flags conservatively. Its C fixture now also asserts R580 size 64, R595 size 72, the offset 64, and a shared BAR1-address prefix. Focused tests pass: source delta 6/6, ABI converter 4/4, R595 producer 7/7.

The fresh full software suite passes **2,576/2,576** in **174.187 s**, exit 0, with identical before/after hashes for all **378 test sources** and all recorded implementation inputs. ## Current increment — P2P capability direction audit and PCIe environment gates — 2026-11-02T15:07Z

Extended [tools/audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py) with a fail-closed R595 UVM P2P consumer-direction audit. Historical PC3 snapshot v37 records the V100 and RTX 5080 under different upstream bridges, ACS redirection on both, and the V100 assigned to vfio-pci; it does not prove current availability or any P2P result. A fresh read-only inventory after PC3 is powered on must establish whether both GPUs are visible to the same Linux kernel and capture IOMMU/ACS/topology before a same-process or transfer test. No IOMMU, ACS, VM, BIOS, or PCI configuration was changed.

## Current increment — GV100 UVM prefetch and PCIe BAR1 aperture — 2026-11-02T14:48Z

This supersedes the 14:36Z checkpoint's pending suite status. The R580.178.04 versus experimental R595.99.02/v15 auditor now covers two path deltas. For prefetch faults, R580 GV100 inherits GP100's Pascal callback, sets `prefetch_fault_supported=true`, and copies it into runtime state. The candidate GV100 table selects Turing callbacks and sets runtime state before dispatch. Both generations' low-level MMU register/RM-toggle helper bodies normalize to the same SHA-256 `8265961e9733a05d6bdfbbb93b90ed16bb3d6c4f02837d1826ed290ddc4f8815`; R580's wrapper asserts the old capability bit and the candidate wrapper does not.

The second path is PCIe BAR1 P2P. R580 returns a peer aperture for every peer link and its Volta CE/PTE helpers do not handle `SYS_NON_COHERENT`. The candidate chooses `SYS_NON_COHERENT` for BAR1 P2P when the RM-reported per-peer PCIe atomic capability is false, adjusts the BAR1 DMA address, encodes the GV100 VER2 PTE aperture value `3` as a system address, and maps the aperture to the Volta CE `NONCOHERENT_SYSMEM` mode. The pinned R580 archive already defines the GV100 PTE aperture field/value and C3B5 noncoherent source/destination modes, which makes the code path statically plausible. The built-in RM-PTE test uses Volta `make_pte`; external allocation PTEs are requested from RM. Neither finding proves actual PCIe atomic behavior or P2P correctness.

[`tools/audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py`](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py) and its 12 focused tests record these paths and fail on changed PTE encoding, missing CE/P2P dispatch, or a callback assigned to the wrong architecture row. ## Current increment — runner cache finalization, mutation races, and OSS test choice — 2026-11-02T10:45Z

## GT200_DEBUGGER exception-mask route statically predicts a null-handle failure — 2026-11-01T10:17Z

This was local-only: no PC, GPU, VM, module, or driver was used. A new cross-layer regression pins the current frontend path, the exact built R595.99.02 and R580.196.07 provider bridge sources, and the corresponding module hashes. The [route audit](../evidence/gt200-debugger-hybrid-control-route-v1-20261101.json), SHA-256 `b521bee737525c869a069565129f02d94926bb58b1b90135e9b5b34ee5664173`, classifies the outcome as analyzed statically with a source-derived predicted result.

Both providers submit the public exception-mask control through NV04_CONTROL using the allocated debugger object. The pinned R580.196.07/R595.99.02 class method maps the public control to an internal debugger control, forwards it through the physical RM API with NV01_NULL_OBJECT, and the pinned RM control path resolves that object through the client's resource map before class dispatch. The same source assigns nonzero client handles from RS_CLIENT_HANDLE_BASE, stores the client proxy under that nonzero handle, and generates nonzero handles for allocations that request zero. The composition therefore predicts NV_ERR_OBJECT_NOT_FOUND on the null internal handle. That status has not been observed at runtime; this remains a static prediction.

The new regression in `tests/test_gt200_debugger_dispatch_source.py` also fixes the test extractor so it skips forward declarations instead of mistaking the next function body for the requested definition. The focused suite now passes **93/93** in 17.971 s; [receipt](../evidence/hybrid-bundle-r595-uvm-regressions-v2-20261101T1016Z.json), SHA-256 `a643b1bb32441e76be075beb0e67b4939ebfcd5ab9cf908c5dd0f29e972614a0`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/hybrid-bundle-r595-uvm-regressions-v2-20261101T1016Z.log`), SHA-256 `7a6e65be681ed6bafc216998056645f64effcc87ce0311b5bda4067aa75572a4`. Passing the test confirms the inspected source composition; it does not validate the control on hardware.

The adapter C sources and headers searched for hInternalClient, hInternalSubdevice, internal-client-handle, and physicalRmApi symbols contain no such provider-side handle route. This bounded search does not prove no other interface exists. The proprietary R580 core was not modified or disassembled. No hardware/runtime validation, module installation, or module load occurred.

**Next executable work:** identify a supported provider interface that can issue this control against a valid backend-owned internal client/subdevice, or show that the CUDA context path tolerates the control failure on an approved canary. Keep the current module bundle uninstalled until that path is resolved or its requirement is disproved.

## Architecture decision — stock R580 cannot serve both target GPUs — 2026-11-01

Official NVIDIA R580.196.07 documentation resolves the key branch ambiguity: NVIDIA ships mutually exclusive proprietary and open kernel-module flavors. Its [kernel-module documentation](https://download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-x86_64/580.196.07/README/kernel_open.html) says the proprietary flavor supports Maxwell, Pascal, Volta, Turing, and later architectures until Blackwell; Blackwell requires the open flavor. The open flavor requires GSP and cannot support pre-Turing GPUs. Therefore R580's package/product list and CUDA API support do not mean one stock R580 kernel-module flavor can initialize both a Volta V100 and an RTX 5080. This is a kernel-backend incompatibility, not a CUDA context-sharing issue.

Keep the first experiment on the already-built split-provider path: R595.99.02 open frontend/RM for Blackwell, the R580.196.07 proprietary Volta provider for GV100, and one R595 UVM consumer with provider ownership kept isolated. This is a custom integration, not an NVIDIA-supported mixed-GPU package. The source/build and symbol audits recorded below establish static composition only; the provider pair has not been loaded or run together. Separate CUDA contexts/devices remain the expected semantics even if one process eventually sees both GPUs. Nouveau remains a possible source of narrowly reusable hardware knowledge, but the inspected evidence does not establish it as a drop-in CUDA/RM Volta provider for this frontend. Reconsider only against a concrete interface and test.

**Decision-changing test:** the fresh PC3 inventory establishes that GV100 is currently passed through to a running VM while the RTX 5080 is attached to the host. Independent stock RTX tests now pass, but no guest login was possible and the two GPUs are not co-visible to one Linux kernel. Do not claim the hybrid acceptance gate. Loading a private module or changing the VM/PCI assignment would interrupt or alter live infrastructure and remains a separately approved canary action.

## Cross-provider UVM P2P remains deliberately fail-closed — 2026-11-01T05:15Z

The mixed-provider bridge now has an executable CPU composition regression against the exact R595.99.02 `uvm_gpu.c` source snapshot (SHA-256 `66881c4ae46461314d41342b2a27df9119d68d68f90d181e3a32670dcc19f590`). `nvUvmInterfaceGetP2PCaps` returns a zeroed capability payload with `p2pLink=NONE` for mixed ownership in either device order and does not call either provider. The pinned R595 static-link discovery skips `NONE` before peer setup; explicit mixed-provider P2P-object creation returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, clears the public handle, and creates no owner record. Mutation controls catch a fabricated link, missing zeroing, cross-provider object success, and an inverted discovery predicate. The source is inspected at [`hybrid580_uvm_callback_bridge.c`](../src/hybrid580_uvm_callback_bridge.c) and the pinned R595 UVM file under `build/research/.../kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_gpu.c`.

The software-suite runner now hashes every regular static file under `tests/`, including compiled C/C++ fixtures, before and after execution. It rejects symlink inputs, skips interpreter caches/special files, aborts if any test-input directory cannot be traversed, and fails the suite if the input hash map changes during the run. The final suite passes **2,382/2,382** in **149.260 s** with zero failures/errors/skips; all **351** test inputs match before and after ([report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261101T051722Z.json), SHA-256 `347e0843778899c7221b3653cd041bb76c81879ee9aa828d7039f63b95cdae0b`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261101T051722Z.log`), SHA-256 `5aa284e41952686067526af49e8a1c3bf69d4adabf003d33d783a68ea890ce6c`). Focused P2P and manifest checks pass **11/11**.

This proves only the tested source/CPU contracts; it does not prove topology, working DMA, peer memory, UVM migration, or P2P behavior on either GPU. Under the selected split-provider architecture, mixed-provider P2P is currently disabled even if PCIe topology might permit it; enabling it requires a provider-neutral mechanism and real hardware tests. The 05:21Z PC3 authentication failure recorded in the historical access receipt was superseded by the 05:33Z direct-PC3 session and RTX tests at the top of this checkpoint. The active QEMU guest still owns the V100; no V100 guest command or same-process hybrid test has run.

## CUDA 13 device-image path for Volta — evidence reconciliation — 2026-11-01T04:36Z

The reusable Volta route is a split toolchain, not native CUDA 13 SM70 support: Clang 23.1.3 with CUDA 13.1.1 headers/device libraries emits PTX 8.8 targeted at `sm_70`; pinned `ptxas` 12.9.86 assembles the SM70 image, with CUDA 12.9 used for device linking. The pinned 744-byte PTX-only image was executed on the stock V100 guest under R580.178.04 / Driver API 13.000: the `hybrid_arch` kernel returned **700**, synchronous and asynchronous numeric tests passed at sizes 1, 31, 256, 4096 and 65537, and cleanup passed. This is a real single-V100 toolchain/runtime result, captured on 2026-10-28; it is not a test of the R595/R580 hybrid modules or same-Linux V100+Blackwell execution. See the [evidence-chain verifier](../evidence/sm70-ptx-jit-runtime-verification-v2-20261028T161312Z.json), SHA-256 `2a36fe51cdc62cf6a4d64ab8e95c9cdb23a3d5461a796006a1b4ffd7be709129`, the [kernel-phase capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-sm70-ptx-jit-kernel-phase-v1-20261028T1618Z.json), SHA-256 `c1aec1f6a26cc3722e0cd3377b71371c213777dd0403b47dc685239b30e61ebf`, and CUDA13-Volta handoff (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `../docs/CUDA13_V100_CURRENT_HANDOFF.md`).

A separate static fatbin inventory reports no embedded SM70 images in the inspected CUDA 13.1 cuBLAS, cuBLASLt, cuFFT, cuSPARSE, cuSOLVER, cuRAND or cudart libraries ([inventory](../evidence/54-cuda13-library-fatbin-arches.json), SHA-256 `29e3f70920e7b297baf280c05ba2eb3ba0cff9be290c7733062ac43820a7ddd8`). This is evidence about those inspected binaries, not proof that every API call fails on Volta. Keep V100 compilation/device-linking on the pinned CUDA 12.9 compatibility lane and Blackwell on CUDA 13.1; CUDA-X 12.9 library execution on V100 still needs its own hardware qualification. Do not claim a single CUDA-X library set or universal CUDA 13 Volta compatibility.

## Hash-pinned PC3 R595 userland preflight — 2026-11-01 UTC

Extended [`tools/run_remote_selected_gpu.py`](../tools/run_remote_selected_gpu.py) (SHA-256 `05dd1a39fff15762ee96c97f9176a64a6ee7b5d6d9159f81fa94f6e84efb444d`) with optional `--expected-libcuda-sha256`. When selected, the PC3-only RTX 5080 runner reads `ldconfig -p`, requires exactly one x86-64 `libcuda.so.1` resolution, resolves a regular file, and compares its SHA-256 against the staged R595.99.02 `libcuda.so.595.99.02` hash `192d4eedb1b39f0568f2b5423d35227d13b32b9025599e1968a9081982da207d` before any CUDA inventory preflight. Static source review confirms the C++ probe reports the resolved library containing its loaded `cuInit` function (`dladdr` followed by `realpath`); the runner requires preflight and acceptance to report that same path, then checks the file's path, size, and hash again. A mismatch, ambiguous loader entry, or mid-run file change fails closed. The option is opt-in; existing unpinned runner behavior is unchanged.

[`tests/test_remote_selected_gpu.py`](../tests/test_remote_selected_gpu.py) (SHA-256 `046af17ba9db397247663ac4df7b35d586430a9ad1e13999695600dffe833fc2`) covers CLI hash validation/forwarding, the exact-hash pass, mismatch before CUDA, ambiguous loader resolution, and library mutation during the probe; focused runner tests pass **70/70** (log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/remote-selected-gpu-focused-v2-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `f2eb324c9f4dc034b212dce9235cf9b3fa32aedad2a356315eb00013bababe2c`). ## R595 per-CPU mm RSS-counter guard — 2026-11-01 UTC

R595.112.07 checks `mm->rss_stat[MM_SHMEMPAGES].counters` before `nv_account_mm_alloc()` allocates bookkeeping and later calls `percpu_counter_add()`. The exact Linux 6.8 target build enables `NV_PERCPU_MM_COUNTER`; the guard therefore compiles into the candidate. If a kernel presents the documented exceptional null backing state, the helper now declines to create optional RSS-accounting work rather than dereferencing a null counter. `nv_account_mm_free(NULL)` is already a no-op, so the GPU-page cleanup path can continue without that accounting record. This is defensive reliability behavior, not a measured performance gain.

Patch [`0019-open-vm-null-percpu-rss-counter-guard.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0019-open-vm-null-percpu-rss-counter-guard.patch) is pinned after the page-pool prototype overlay because both touch `nv-vm.c`. Its preimage and postimage are hash-verified; it applies with no fuzz or offset. Two tests compare the function against pinned R595.112.07, assert the null guard precedes allocation and counter access, and reject a modified preimage. The builder test suite passes **19/19**. The complete pinned CPU/software suite, including these tests, passes **2,331/2,331**, zero failures/errors/skips, in **148.219 s** ([report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-memory-guards-v4-20261101.json), SHA-256 `950e04801ff67f4be469e250dcb198f93127924b14173109f0e57db845a2aa19`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r595-memory-guards-v4-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `3d119724c2bcc4e573aa8c0c74906dbd6b0357857f60519a3995340602d1ae2d`). The exact-target full module build passes ([receipt](../evidence/r595-open-linux68-memory-guards-v3-20261101.json), SHA-256 `a7768362bf930bd9aa9d6f2a7ca56bb1c8c12af4186c6955daaa0a4740c47c35`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-open-linux68-memory-guards-v3-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `8b29c05e9e08b2d82dffba4d025e73307ff9cbb264ee698bdb6fb5fa24a75cd4`). Built module SHA-256 `f606065d34d789875bcc6bfa663f633bb0093d6e3158c10c2373391b7e2e68c3`; semantic `Module.symvers` ABI SHA-256 remains `285db44e3d654091312049a2bb94d3b312ed806ec0169ec830a7362d6c56a549`. One known Linux-header `fops_get(&nvidia_fops)` warning remains. No module was installed or loaded; no remote PC or GPU was accessed.

The remaining kernel-open differences were checked for duplication and target scope: R595.112.07's `os-mlock.c` page-backed-PFN guard is already represented by [`0013-open-pfn-backed-io-guard.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0013-open-pfn-backed-io-guard.patch), so it was not duplicated. Its `NV_PGPROT_READ_ONLY` adjustment is inside the ARM64 branch and is not applicable to the x86_64 Linux 6.8 target. NVLink capability-FD validation and display/NVSwitch changes are not part of this three-patch compute-path increment.

## R595 read-only mmap protection ordering — 2026-11-01 UTC

R595.112.07 moves the `NV_PROTECT_WRITEABLE` check in `nvidia_mmap_helper()` to before any BAR, peer-I/O, or system-memory mapping. R595.99.02 instead set `vm_page_prot` and cleared `VM_WRITE`/`VM_MAYWRITE` after those mapping functions returned. Because the mapping helpers consume the VMA's protection while creating mappings, setting protections first preserves the intended read-only permission at PTE installation; Linux's memory API documents `pgprot` as the protection used by `remap_pfn_range()` and notes that drivers may need to adjust `vma->vm_page_prot` before mapping ([Linux MM API](https://docs.kernel.org/core-api/mm-api.html)). This is a protection-order correctness change, not a performance claim.

Patch [`0018-open-mmap-readonly-before-pte-install.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0018-open-mmap-readonly-before-pte-install.patch) carries only that move, retaining the already-replayed R580 power-transition access guard and avoiding the R595.112.07 ARM64-specific PTE change on our x86_64 target. Hash-pinned preimage/postimage apply with no fuzz or offset. Regression checks assert read-only setup and both write-flag clears precede peer-I/O and system-memory mapping and that the R580 PM guard remains. The full builder test module passes **17/17**. The final pinned CPU/software suite, including the strengthened ordering checks, passes **2,329/2,329**, zero failures/errors/skips, in **148.391 s** ([report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-mmap-protection-v3-20261101.json), SHA-256 `e85c2f514a216fb98eb4f7777283ea84fa2fc9a49b0601d202f752edb5b7f8c9`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r595-mmap-protection-v3-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `178285e809d3f8ca7e6e0dd048f7028314f5295197bdad5feab2b598aa03c6a7`). Full-source R595.99.02 build for `6.8.0-147-generic` passes ([receipt](../evidence/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-mmap-v2-20261101.json), SHA-256 `c2e781d6507f27e4f75af4aeb5eb323fc308c2c4d0748d9c373186abfa09c863`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-mmap-v2-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `8e2a4de1272f95ef3ecfd2a527eb500f745950344038531d694f640b2a3cfd12`). Built module SHA-256 `e8c34ea0a9a66eaadc243e3a03d6509a502ee9f8c66de5d801c047e782d19731`; semantic `Module.symvers` ABI SHA-256 remains `285db44e3d654091312049a2bb94d3b312ed806ec0169ec830a7362d6c56a549`. The existing single `fops_get(&nvidia_fops)` warning remains. No module was installed/loaded and no GPU or remote PC was accessed. Hardware behavior and protection-fault tests remain unvalidated.

## R595 dma-buf scatter-gather unmap correction — 2026-11-01 UTC

The R595.112.07 `nv-dmabuf.c` delta changes `dma_unmap_sg_attrs()` from `sgt->nents` to `sgt->orig_nents`. Linux's DMA API requires unmap to receive the original count passed to `dma_map_sg_attrs()`, even when the DMA layer merges entries and returns fewer mapped segments. R595 already maps with `orig_nents`, stores the mapped count in `nents`, and iterates mapped entries using `nents`; only the unmap count was wrong. This is a generic DMA correctness fix, not a measured V100 performance improvement ([Linux DMA API](https://docs.kernel.org/core-api/dma-api.html)).

Added exact hash-pinned patch [`0017-open-dmabuf-unmap-original-nents.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0017-open-dmabuf-unmap-original-nents.patch), wired into the clean Linux 6.8 build with preimage/postimage verification and zero fuzz/offset. Three focused regressions pass; the complete builder test module passes **15/15**. Full R595.99.02 Open build for `6.8.0-147-generic` passes with GCC 13.3 ([receipt](../evidence/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-original-nents-v1-20261101.json), SHA-256 `a9c5d484174831f426dc9c53279f88f3d60f8b44843bb3b5f7756d41b8384669`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-original-nents-v1-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `1b4e21f8a7886d2c1f877bf36cd6a20e11cb04847301f3405cf20aeb17dc3d64`). The full pinned CPU/software suite then passes **2,327/2,327**, with zero failures/errors/skips, in **147.646 s** ([report](../evidence/software-verification-dmabuf-r595-v1-20261101.json), SHA-256 `03608f3acabc70cd936a6ffdd4a9a62ed296b348227da64ed18ca1aa3ba061a7`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-dmabuf-r595-v1-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `7f678e14e14b3f8608eab95237f22638d0885fa04c0c543b8cb5d30754b9ed18`). Built module SHA-256 `81caf24a6b5e430def1945dd96927ff1af13cab9a09e9ae88070361a6e8b2992`; semantic `Module.symvers` ABI SHA-256 remains `285db44e3d654091312049a2bb94d3b312ed806ec0169ec830a7362d6c56a549`. One known Linux-header `fops_get(&nvidia_fops)` warning remains. The patch changes no symbols. This is compiled-source and CPU-test evidence only: no module install/load, GPU, or remote PC was used. Hardware DMA behavior, P2P, hybrid CUDA, performance, and endurance remain unvalidated.

## PC3 direct-only dynamic VM/IOMMU audit — 2026-11-01 UTC

The VM assignment auditor no longer depends on the 2026-10-29 BDF/group snapshot or a PC6-sourced sudo credential. `tools/audit_pc3_vm_gpu_assignment.py` now refuses to run unless given both `--direct-pc3` and a fresh, mode-0600 collector snapshot under `evidence/` whose collector/script hashes validate and whose transport provenance is direct PC3 key plus `sudo -n`. It derives the RTX 5080 BDF and IOMMU group from that snapshot, resolves the live group through `/sys/bus/pci/devices/<BDF>/iommu_group`, cross-checks NVIDIA GPU identities and the VM's VFIO hostdev assignment, and emits a private report without rebind, VM, driver, or boot changes.

The final v4 auditor source SHA-256 is `2bd864fd2ebf8bbe459856ba713b283bcb67c0248195e2a7f268bf53e1adcbf5`; its test source SHA-256 is `255c772d0e0c35610a5a8da42b8c459fd5e4db0a1c96f044cfb5bf950cbe518c`. Next action when PC3 is online: run `tools/collect_pc3_canary_host_state.py --direct-pc3` to a new evidence path, then pass that exact path to `tools/audit_pc3_vm_gpu_assignment.py --direct-pc3 --host-snapshot ...`. ## R595 large P2P page-table backport and exact Linux 6.8 build — 2026-10-31 UTC

The pinned R595.99.02 versus R595.112.07 comparison covers 2,172 C/H files: 93 differ (76 in `src/nvidia`, 17 in `kernel-open`), no files were added or removed, and all 213 UVM files are identical. The targeted R595.112.07 `nvos_create_alloc()` fix handles allocations above `INT_MAX` using zeroed `nv_vmalloc()` and matching sized `nv_vfree()`. Patch [`0015-open-large-page-table-vmalloc.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0015-open-large-page-table-vmalloc.patch) includes the three required compatibility hunks: allocator/free behavior, the `nv_vmalloc(size, gfp_t flags)` wrapper, and the `GFP_KERNEL` call. The first Kbuild exposed the changed wrapper signature; the complete three-hunk backport then built successfully. Reproduce the release comparison with [`audit_r595_open_release_delta.py`](../tools/audit_r595_open_release_delta.py); [source-delta report](../evidence/r595-open-kernel-source-delta-v1-20261031.json), SHA-256 `45aaa0ed93da7a84b7b58e96689e92fd562a7a6a557a2ed3f7cc8dd6680b5d255`.

The full R595.99.02 `nvidia`/`nvidia-uvm` source compiles against exact `6.8.0-147-generic` headers with pinned GCC 13.3. The replay exposed missing prototypes for two frontend client-share/sync-boost exports; patch [`0049-private-open-control-export-prototypes.patch`](../patches/r580.178.04/0049-private-open-control-export-prototypes.patch) adds their exact declarations. A separate warning cleanup, [`0016-open-page-pool-prototypes.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0016-open-page-pool-prototypes.patch), gives four file-local page-pool helpers internal linkage and puts the two cross-file page-pool lifecycle declarations in the shared Linux header. The all-44-patch [replay v5](../evidence/r595-syncboost-replay-v5.json), SHA-256 `2c5b2e78e036dbe13bb1c72e9efdc2709c1624347f503ffff7743be087c26727`, remains unchanged and does not mutate its source tree.

One intermediate v5 build compiled successfully but its receipt validation compared the previous overlay’s intermediate header hash after the page-pool overlay had correctly changed that same file. ## VMM teardown dependency hardening — 2026-10-31 UTC

Static review of the prior probe found that it attempted `cuMemRelease` and `cuMemAddressFree` after `cuMemUnmap` failed, and continued into the reverse direction despite incomplete cleanup. The current VMM probe stops the dependent cleanup chain after an unmap failure, records the skipped calls, marks the overall result failed, and skips the reverse-direction run after any cleanup failure. A CPU fake-`libcuda` regression injects an unmap error and verifies that only one direction is attempted and neither dependent cleanup API is called.

The new versioned build preserves earlier build artifacts and pins: `v3 binary` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/vmm-peer-probe-v3-20261031/hybrid_cuda_vmm_peer_probe`), SHA-256 `3ebe23609679621035c14f2eec895e4df5df0cbe9bd8e00cddde23e8b9cd92e0`; manifest (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/vmm-peer-probe-v3-20261031/build-manifest.json`), SHA-256 `52daa929819bc3b82407a6f3fa16824dc9083a45ac8cd81510ded7b87f8e2e5d`; source SHA-256 `5af98eb2e547db18d793121a78d8c09e985e67e83d6f295b4f666321428e012e`. The exact source, build, runner, test, and limitation record is in [software hardening receipt v5](../evidence/vmm-peer-probe-software-hardening-v5-20261031.json), SHA-256 `d78450b825853f8dffc836c83b2426051717901ae434aa0ae308d41eb6943856`.

## CUDA VMM cross-generation peer-read discriminator — initial build, superseded — 2026-10-31 UTC

**Implemented and compiled; hardware-unvalidated.** Added `src/hybrid_cuda_vmm_peer_probe.cpp` as a separate, opt-in test-only discriminator for explicit CUDA VMM permissions between exact SM70 and SM120 PCI devices. It creates one context per device, allocates pinned physical memory on the source, maps it into the process VA, assigns source read-write and peer read-only access with `cuMemSetAccess`, verifies both with `cuMemGetAccess`, then launches the pinned numeric kernel on the other GPU in each direction and checks the output. It does not call `cuCtxEnablePeerAccess`, change production UVM/P2P routing, or claim a DMA route. Build: `build manifest` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/vmm-peer-probe-v1-20261031/build-manifest.json`), binary SHA-256 `4aaa7341d5314960a5dd3a494a9915cd4c7e0c0bf1158d02d4c54b69e47b791e`, source SHA-256 `cfd71b68d16c8931dbdf0891b8778daee6d90dff0331cabd57f7d9744fb6440f`, pinned SM70/SM120 fatbin SHA-256 `9b6c772590c0e7ce6a95e36fb637b769eceef69856979761d739028d8b45675f`. The C++ source compiled with GCC 15 and `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`; the builder verifies both cubin images and rejects direct `libcuda` linkage. `readelf` shows no `libcuda.so` dependency, `nm` shows no unresolved CUDA Driver API imports, and GCC `-fanalyzer -Werror` emitted no diagnostics. The mode-0600 [static review receipt v3](../evidence/cuda-vmm-peer-static-review-v3-20261031.json), SHA-256 `2d7fc24b6ca2fce59d2774fa67fe6a178a1634cd7029e2f9d8d3f632ecde98cd`, covers help, missing arguments, malformed capability/count parsing, and valid preflight JSON; these paths reject before GPU initialization.

The runner pins binary/fatbin/manifest identities independently, exact PCI IDs and model names, and the reported CUDA VMM mode; it refuses an active compute process on either target, strips `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, and `LD_AUDIT`, identifies/hashes the loaded `libcuda`, VMM report contract, and runner, bounds runtime, handles byte/text timeout output, and writes fresh mode-0600 evidence with exclusive/no-follow creation. Contract and safety tests now pass **28/28**. The prior full pinned CPU/software suite passed **2,278/2,278**, zero failures/errors/skips, in **142.205 s**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261031T220723Z.json), SHA-256 `0ec663533c18ac5f2e28b6830626052949db06c34b23780f78ac77ba89733f77`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261031T220723Z.log`), SHA-256 `3d194d3567504abbd8725c3a133b40d78e153de071330aec076b281ba908c235`. The latest full-suite rerun is recorded in the trust-anchor correction above.

The user reported PC3 was off and planned to power it on in approximately ten minutes. Two single targeted `tailscale ping --c 1 100.90.188.53` attempts after that interval returned no reply; the subsequent SSH connection to alias `pc3` timed out before `hostname` ran. No remote command or GPU test ran, and no other host was contacted. This does not establish PC3 or VM power state. See mode-0600 [access probe v11](../evidence/pc3-access-probe-v11-20261031.json), [v12](../evidence/pc3-access-probe-v12-20261031.json), and [latest v13](../evidence/pc3-access-probe-v13-20261031.json), SHA-256 `01e685bc731a718f606a8deed8b169a565deff9fb8b689dc9808f9b4a7eaf887`. No fresh GPU inventory was observed. The project remains compiled/software-contract evidence only; VMM behavior, device initialization, the same-Linux pair, CUDA kernels, UVM, P2P, performance, and stability remain unvalidated. When PC3 becomes reachable, first refresh its actual Linux/VM boundary, GPU PCI identities, stock driver/libcuda, and workload ownership read-only; run the VMM discriminator only if that same kernel sees both target GPUs and the runner confirms them idle.

The official [CUDA 13.1 VMM guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/13.1.0/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/virtual-memory-management.html#access-rights) confirms that mapping alone does not grant device access, `cuMemSetAccess` must set permissions for source and accessing devices, and teardown proceeds through unmap, release, and address free. The [peer-context API reference](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/13.1.0/cuda-driver-api/group__CUDA__PEER__ACCESS.html) limits the `cuCtxEnablePeerAccess` same-major immediate-access guarantee to equal-major devices. The probe follows the documented VMM sequence and deliberately treats mixed-major SM70/SM120 compatibility as an empirical question; this documentation is not hardware proof.

## Opt-in CUDA peer-transfer diagnostic — 2026-10-31 UTC

Probe v3 adds a separate peer-memory read after the original copy checks. For each enabled direction, it initializes a source-context allocation, launches `hybrid_peer_read` on the destination GPU with the source pointer, and checks all 1,024 returned 32-bit values. The direction-level report distinguishes `direct_access_enabled`, `direct_kernel_attempted`, and `direct_kernel_passed` from synchronous/asynchronous copy integrity. A copy failure no longer skips the peer-kernel test; a failed copy still fails the total diagnostic. `peer-memory-verified` indicates that a kernel dereferenced the peer allocation, not that a specific DMA/NVLink/PCIe route was used; every report still records `direct_dma_attested=false`. Cross-provider production P2P remains fail-closed.

The pinned build and its SM70/SM120 images are in `probe build v3` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/probe-peer-transfer-v3-20261031/build-manifest.json`): probe SHA-256 `d7cdbd528cbf4665a1ffe2ca0d5792e8e70de98cb88fd368f4a1e496abdcac13`, fatbin SHA-256 `9b6c772590c0e7ce6a95e36fb637b769eceef69856979761d739028d8b45675f`, host-source SHA-256 `59b6b057e7b122b4e3d9882c4304e9818660ec4dd2f8a13d268958fb97a26384`. `cuobjdump --dump-elf-symbols` reports `hybrid_peer_read` as a global entry point in both images (symbol log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/cuda-peer-memory-fatbin-symbols-v3-20261031.log`)). A separate `-Werror` compile emitted no diagnostics and matched the pinned binary; [receipt](../evidence/cuda-peer-memory-werror-build-v1-20261031.json). Focused host-only tests pass **163/163**, including one-way access, no-access copies, direct kernel read/failure, independent copy failure and cleanup, no-peer-API compilation, and rejection of incomplete direction reports: log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/cuda-peer-memory-focused-tests-v3-final2-20261031.log`), SHA-256 `b7a3c338cc7821cc132f405bcd71e38e4e248d1ba68679bcf717ba50db180a7b`. The latest suite result is recorded in the VMM increment above. Full build and qualification details are in [probe evidence v3](../evidence/cuda-peer-memory-probe-v3-20261031.json).

## Latest verified increment — deterministic R580/R595 GV100 PTE differential (2026-10-30 UTC)

The host MMU harness now compiles the pinned R580 `make_pte_volta` beside the exact R595 v15 candidate and compares outputs across **8,448 deterministic vectors**: SYS, SYS_NON_COHERENT, VID, PEER_0–PEER_7; all three valid protections; all four accepted flag combinations; aligned addresses including zero, one page, high address bits, the maximum bounded address, and seeded samples. The noncoherent oracle starts from the R580 SYS PTE and replaces only the aperture field using the generated GV100 encoding. This preserves the VALID bit and deliberately uses the shifted field mask.

The exact v15 candidate passes those vectors, the existing **32 fixed numerical vectors**, and **2 assertion guards**. A source mutant that toggles the read-only bit only when physical address bit 56 is set is rejected by the differential test. See [candidate run v2](../evidence/volta-mmu-host-harness-r595-v15-v2-20261030T0535Z.json), SHA-256 `36c1979252f182280ca9329124faf5a5d7d50e8fa4ddc5020b948d4dbd74434b`, and log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/volta-mmu-host-harness-r595-v15-v2-20261030T0535Z.log`), SHA-256 `a9562017d2eb468cbc62f85fe3fe99b57be37c9197b7643fd8c32a3e7241fe8f`. Focused audit/harness tests pass **14/14**.

The fresh full pinned suite after this change passes **2,230 tests**, zero failures/errors/skips, in **142.286 s**, exit 0. [Report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261030T053556Z.json), SHA-256 `81d69dd08486a58452f68ee253150baa3f64bb9c26edbaf8724ae55bedbf13c6`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261030T053556Z.log`), SHA-256 `a29b1df7d0d06ab66c6a264365f761c99d85102b22bc80b58b0a7b3b7286a588`.

## Latest verified increment — R595 v15 Volta MMU adapter profile (2026-10-30 UTC)

## Previous verified increment — canonical reproducible shim build (2026-10-30 UTC)

The process-local R580 P2P fault-injection shim remains limited to the isolated PC3 stock-driver A/B runner. It identifies `/dev/nvidiactl` by character-device identity, filters only three compile-time-pinned RM ioctl encodings, tracks successful BAR1 P2P objects before denying selected operation families, serializes allocation/control/free tracking windows, and invalidates evidence on tracking overflow. The runner confines the shim to its child, rejects mixed capture modes, checks each denial diagnostic, and never treats an injected nonzero CUDA probe exit as success.

Independent review found a variadic ABI defect in the first-call forwarding path: a C helper re-issued an unrelated ioctl with its raw third argument typed as `unsigned long`. The cold path now calls a resolver while saving RAX and all integer argument registers, restores them, and tail-jumps directly to the resolved `RTLD_NEXT` symbol. The fake scalar ioctl reads the actual promoted `int`; separate fresh processes exercise scalar-first and no-argument-first forwarding. A source-contract regression pins the save/restore order and excludes the old variadic C relay.

The first canonical runs exposed a portability defect: the builder assumed a 438 MiB SDK source tree stored only in the local mirror. The canonical tree already contained the matching R580.196.07 source headers in `build/experiments/r58019607-proprietary-module-build-v4-20261029/source`. The builder now selects only a candidate whose required include directories exist and whose six pinned header hashes match, and records the selected relative path in its report. Initial failures are preserved in canonical run v1 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `control/runs/rm-p2p-fault-canonical-focused-v1-20261030.log`) and v2 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `control/runs/rm-p2p-fault-canonical-focused-v2-20261030.log`).

Canonical builder run v8 selected that existing source tree and reproduced the 16,432-byte shim exactly: SHA-256 `5b36f90c12f56d3ca1956b8aac61db3d8df9306a96deb1cfd4a81c52683e298e`; [build receipt](../evidence/rm-p2p-fault-injector-build-v8-canonical-20261030.json). Selector tests cover skipping an incomplete primary, selecting a valid pinned fallback, and failing closed when every candidate hash mismatches. A synthetic `dlsym` interposer forces `RTLD_NEXT` ioctl resolution to fail and verifies the assembly cold path returns `-1` with `ENOSYS`. The fixture's bootstrap symbol was changed from `GLIBC_2.34` to the older x86-64 `GLIBC_2.2.5` baseline; the focused UBSan injector/runner tests pass **13/13** from the canonical tree after that change. All **110/110** runner contracts pass. Latest focused canonical log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `control/runs/rm-p2p-fault-canonical-focused-v6-20261030.log`); latest full canonical runner log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `control/runs/rm-p2p-runner-contract-canonical-full-v2-20261030.log`). GCC `-fanalyzer -Werror` remains clean for unchanged production C/assembly inputs, and the four-file Python source compile check passes.

The independent cold-path review checked register preservation, stack alignment, direct tail-jump, ENOSYS handling, fresh-process scalar-first forwarding, and the promoted `int` fixture. A resolver-failure follow-up review verified the interposition behavior against its GLIBC_2.34 fixture; the checked-in fixture was then rebased to GLIBC_2.2.5 and the canonical 13-test suite passed again. The source-root fallback review found no blocker; negative and positive selection regressions close its coverage suggestions. The access probe is recorded in [pc3-access-probe-v1](../evidence/pc3-access-probe-v1-20261030.json). ## Objective and status
Build one coherent Linux CUDA stack that can initialize and run CUDA on Volta V100 and Blackwell RTX 5080 in one process. **Mission incomplete.** The R595.99.02/R580.196.07 candidate has source-analysis, CPU-test, exact-target-build, and static-module-closure evidence only; no experimental module has been installed or loaded. Separately, the pinned v10 probe passed SM120 synchronous/asynchronous numerical kernels plus managed-memory/prefetch on the stock PC1 RTX 5090/R595.99.02. Same-host V100+RTX 5080 execution, hybrid-driver CUDA/UVM, P2P, concurrent GPU overlap, performance, endurance, and recovery remain unvalidated.

## Canonical project and selected experiment
Canonical workspace: `/run/media/pc1/Infrastructure/Naos/60_travaux/cuda13-volta-compat-20260927/driver-hybrid`. The current experimental route is the R595.99.02 Open frontend/UVM with a R580.196.07 Volta provider and R580.196.07-derived GV100 HAL. The frontend and UVM target Linux `6.8.0-147-generic`, GCC 13.3. The frontend build now consumes the exact Linux 6.8 R595 Open and R580.196.07 provider `Module.symvers`; a prior build report used the wrong R580 7.0 symbol-table path and was rejected by the bundle auditor.

The bridge in [`hybrid580_uvm_callback_bridge.c`](../src/hybrid580_uvm_callback_bridge.c) translates the R580/R595 `sysmemLink` enum and preserves R580 Bug 5262806 by clamping the legacy provider's `accessCntrBufferCount` to one. Exact R580.196.07 source performs that clamp in UVM after `nvUvmInterfaceGetGpuInfo`; R595 keeps the analogous workaround in its Blackwell HAL. The callback is owner-routed through the legacy provider. Direct pass-through from the R595 Open provider remains unchanged.

## Current local increment — GT200_DEBUGGER allocation evidence (2026-11-01 UTC)
PC3 is powered off per the user's latest instruction. No host, VM, or other-PC connection was attempted in this increment. Continue local-only work until the user-indicated ten-minute interval has elapsed and PC3 is confirmed available; do not infer that it is online. No driver/module installation, VM operation, or PCI reassignment was performed.

Added a narrowly scoped `GT200_DEBUGGER` observer for the fixed 12-byte `NV83DE_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` payload, with raw handles and pointers omitted, entry/exit payload consistency checks, and fail-closed parameter-size handling. The option is wired through the selected-GPU acceptance runner and remote transport contract. A CPU-only end-to-end acceptance fixture now exercises the forwarding and evidence contract.

Static R580.178.04, R580.196.07, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10 sources agree on the exact `GT200_DEBUGGER` resource-entry block (SHA-256 `59c50239f2437ae512846c8a03ebbcfde7f7d3dfdf9b7cd3a2b0810b50aac537`): `KernelSMDebuggerSession`, outer parent `NV01_DEVICE_0`, required `NV83DE_ALLOC_PARAMETERS`, and dual-client lock. The constructor resolves nested `hClass3dObject` inside `hAppClient`, requires a graphics object, validates security/access and same-GPU ownership, then attaches to that object's channel/context/subdevice. The best reconciliation (inference) is that the SDK's phrase “child of compute or 3D” describes the logical debuggee association; the RM parent remains the device. The matching RM lookup uses nested `hAppClient` as the second client. The class header hash is `44835665d85dd315f6cb398e6f4a016eea4756acf6088f0c7e800c22c159d21a`. This establishes static interface agreement only; it does not prove live handle ownership or hybrid allocation success.

One robustness delta is now pinned: the R580.196.07 source snapshot initializes the internal client before registering the debug session and invokes `_ksmdbgssnCleanup` on constructor failure. The R580.178.04, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10 constructor snapshots retain registration-before-initialization and have no equivalent constructor unwind helper. This identifies a small port candidate, not a demonstrated leak or a change yet applied to the hybrid candidate.

Fresh local verification passed **209 tests** across the static audit, new capture contract, observer, remote selected-GPU runner, and acceptance runner. Evidence: [`gt200-debugger-integration-v3-20261101T030740Z.json`](../evidence/gt200-debugger-integration-v3-20261101T030740Z.json), report SHA-256 `29399001664f86125fc3b3af6e4cf39272b30f40ec4fdec8338e4658a62851aa`; private log `control/runs/gt200-debugger-integration-v3-20261101T030740Z.log` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer), SHA-256 `13c071ea9b874860e8314c6117630e40a16d17ca971b1eb425133e2a1602fe9f`. The expanded static report is [`gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v4-20261101T0307Z.json`](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v4-20261101T0307Z.json), SHA-256 `034709565f6f522c23523aa2b48ac18314fed2f6ae5f50b29490b5f42c6fe9be`.

## Current local increment — targeted R580 RM decompilation and debugger-route audit (2026-11-01 UTC)

PC3 remains powered off per the user's latest instruction. This increment used only the local canonical checkout; no remote PC, VM, GPU, installed driver, or module was accessed or changed.

The prior tool inventory missed Ghidra because it is bundled outside `PATH`. Ghidra 12.1.4 at `build/tools/ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC/` ran headlessly with OpenJDK 21.0.12.1. REA is not on `PATH` and no REA MCP tool is available; no engine was installed. Two bounded imports analyzed the hash-pinned R580.178.04 open-module `nv-kernel.o_binary` (ET_REL, x86-64, 17,512,968 bytes; SHA-256 `5e9219a4a263315d20757159852b35be61dfd2ce75dcd2ca90b1cd1764d6bb8a`) and decompiled nine selected symbols. The private project and pseudocode remain under the mode-0700 experiment directory; outputs are explicitly labeled pseudocode, not NVIDIA source. Ghidra receipt (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/r580-gt200-debugger-route-ghidra-v1-20261101.json`), SHA-256 `0d6e20c8b07a956732cfc97b3898bb127d4303a2881c1cd38bf18faca6516b9f`.

The targeted result confirms in the R580 object that `ksmdbgssnGetInternalObjectHandle_IMPL` returns zero and the debugger internal-control method forwards through `gpuresInternalControlForward_IMPL`. Cross-version source checks pin the same forwarding, object-handle, GPU dispatch, and Resource Server lookup function bodies across R580.178.04, R580.196.07, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10. `_gpuRmApiControl` has a direct fast path only for the GPU's exact internal client/subdevice pair; its fallback carries the requested handles to Resource Server. The server resolves `hObject` in the client's map, while standard resource-handle allocation rejects zero. This is a conditional static failure prediction if the generated internal forwarder is invoked on the ordinary path; a live call trace is still needed to rule out a wrapper or alternate dispatch.

The existing GT200_DEBUGGER audit now records these cross-version route contracts in v7, and regression coverage includes branch pins and a comment/string-safe C function extractor. The full CPU/software suite passes **2,429/2,429**, exit 0, in **169.748 s**; all **357** test-source and **76** implementation-input hash maps match before and after. [Audit](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v7-20261101.json), SHA-256 `06acb741d685025a7652540bf8168305abad13aa4452ea0c0ee6cbfce83878ae`; [suite report](../evidence/software-verification-gt200-debugger-control-route-v1-20261101.json), SHA-256 `560b1b211c49394453ab42dbf828214534be6922bafc72874a066f48e03f7ed7`; private log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-gt200-debugger-control-route-v1-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `4d1861bc6336e6fad63cdce09303046bf1674867c5a20ac1565d8d664d02d92a`.

No driver source was changed or module installed. ## Current local increment — Volta fault-ring overflow status and assertion modes — 2026-11-03 02:20 UTC

Cross-checked the source-pinned Linux v7.0 Nouveau lifecycle analysis against NVIDIA's open-GPU reference at commit `9fdf5c4062007929d9f4e6cbad9c9771fe61b880`. The GV100 register map defines GET PTR[19:0], GET OVERFLOW bit 31, GETPTR_CORRUPTED bit 30, and corresponding PUT status bits. The Volta `read_get` callback asserts the raw GET word is below `max_faults` before extracting PTR. Combined with the official field map, that predicts a debug assertion if an overflow status bit is read alongside a valid pointer. The pinned R580 and R595 `uvm_common.h` snapshots disable `UVM_ASSERT` in Release; this makes the finding a debug-assertion edge, not evidence of a release-path memory or index defect.

Extended `tools/run_volta_fault_ring_host_harness.py` to hash-pin both `uvm_common.h` files, exercise the GET OVERFLOW debug assertion for each source, and compile a separate test-only release-assert model that still returns PTR=3. The focused `tests.test_volta_fault_ring_host_harness` passes. Original callback/service coverage remains **244/244**; the new independent release probes pass **2/2**; the expected assertion guards are **8/8** across both sources. The path-bearing local v4 report has SHA-256 `9e37ef239fdf7ab24b13cfeeb63e743c5aca69c92f804d7aa2e1342e3c8a4d15`, and its private log SHA-256 is `c70b45c9afdfb4e1dff33ca23b51f9472bb08c1c8470c3aaf1462f3e03d31764`; both remain outside the Git snapshot. Updated [reverse-engineering notes](REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md) and [validation notes](VALIDATION.md).

The exact R595.99.02 v15 Volta-overlay object command was re-preprocessed: it contains no `-DDEBUG` and emits `UVM_IS_DEBUG()=0`. Its pinned object (`1d72bf4103055fa784896f575e3882dac9561026de3203cf84bc05a16e94a181`) and module (`21c6b16c4fc64d81b4326866ea708b8229735035724fcc8bfb95788b8c442b1b`) were compiled, not installed or loaded. The sanitized [assert-build receipt](../evidence/r595-volta-assert-build-mode-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `7b7d4632c60a6a1516b6be8c6b69695318e073b8cefce9cbb5ff9f08e603979f`, ties those bytes to the R580-derived Volta source and R595 assertion header. This confirms the host Release model matches that older compiled overlay.

At the start of this increment, the stock R595.112.07 hybrid UVM source build was a 446-file tree without `uvm_volta_fault_buffer.c` (baseline tree SHA-256 `438de403568dc865f4080112f28b37d91794c3598086c1f75287180dec80328f`, baseline module SHA-256 `b467e051c4af84011d512859751c20d0ef042e5c24d3281fbfb248173c18f4f2`).
